Title: Paradoxa Ducenta Octoginta, Das ist: Zweyhundert vnnd Achtzig Wunderred, gleichsam Rhäterschafft, auß der H. Schrifft, so vor allem fleisch vnglaüblich vnnd vnwar seind, doch wider der gantzen welt wahn vnd achtung, gewiß vnd war: Item aller in Gott Philosophierenden Christen Rechte, Göttliche Philosophey, vnd Teütsche Theologey
Creator: Sebastian Franck
Publisher: Pforzheim: Georg Rab (Corvinus), 1559 ("M. D. LIX." at file line 106); a later printing of the work first published in 1534
Publication Date: 1559
Rights: Public Domain (published 1559; public domain by date; host carries the Creative Commons Public Domain Mark 1.0)
Language: deu
Source: Internet Archive item bub_gb_6_s7AAAAcAAJ, file bub_gb_6_s7AAAAcAAJ_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for family-of-love-and-spiritualists-franck-niclaes), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: licenseurl http://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/mark/1.0/ (contributor Bavarian State Library, Google-digitised).
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long-s to s normalisation (24056 occurrences). Rights basis restated: a 1559 Pforzheim imprint, the year read in the OCR of the title page, is public domain by date, and the host marks the item Public Domain.
Content note: The whole print: title page, Franck's Vorred (from about file line 550), then the 280 numbered Paradoxa with their Latin and German theses and Franck's expositions, with the running head "Paradoxa" and printed leaf numbers, and a register. Franck's central statement of his spiritualist theology (the invisible church, the inner Word, Scripture as letter). SECOND WITNESS for exact wording: Google-digitised OCR of a Fraktur print, sampled at five points and found garbled in part (about 0.75 of tokens read as words; "f" for long s in many words, broken words); use it to locate passages and verify against the page images (Mark's OCR ruling, 2026-09-25). A 1558 printing by the same press (item bub_gb_4fk7AAAAcAAJ) reads slightly worse (0.74). The 1909 Jena edition in modernised German (item paradoxa00franuoft, ed. Heinrich Ziegler) is set in Fraktur and reads worse still (0.66). No public-domain English translation exists (Furcha's 280 Paradoxes, 1986, is in copyright).
